# Porting the hub to Rust

Decision (2026-11-04): the backend moves to Rust. Bend keeps only the small, pure
rules worth keeping in it: policy, validation and state-transition rules. Everything
that changes over time goes to Rust: the event store, sockets, files, processes,
broadcast and caches.

Scope: the `gpui` branch and the Backplane GPUI project only. `main` and the live
Backplane stay untouched, and nothing merges to main without the person's OK.

Why, from `NATIVE-BEND.md`: native Bend is 1.4-2.7x faster than Bend on JS and uses far
less memory, but stays 3.6-95x slower than plain TypeScript on hub hot paths, and the
whole hub cannot be emitted to C (212 JS-only effect defs; the emit exceeds 8 GiB).

## Target

```
GPUI, iOS, Android  ──  today's protocol, unchanged  ──  backplane-hub (Rust, native/host)
                                                         ├ event store, windowed history
                                                         ├ broadcast, fan-out, view sessions
                                                         ├ threads, sidebar status
                                                         ├ agent processes
                                                         ├ files, git, KiCad IPC
                                                         └ calls: Bend rules; presenter
```

`events.jsonl` keeps its format: persisted events stay decodable forever, and the Rust
hub reads a log the Bend hub wrote, and the reverse.

## Native clients only

Decision (2026-11-04): no web page. The clients are GPUI, SwiftUI and Compose. The Rust
hub serves no web client and has no `renderer: web`, so `src/web/view.bend` and the
web host are out of the port. The web UI remains only as the reference for how the
native apps should look, and Electron remains only as the baseline to measure against.

## The presentation layer

The hub draws what clients show: GPUI and the phones get a screen model (`Render.mobile`)
from client.bend, screen.bend and hubs.bend, about 22,000 lines, far from small. It is
pure, so it stays in Bend (native, no JS) while Rust owns everything that changes.

**Page-based design (revised 2026-11-05, from measurements; docs/gpui/HUB-M1.md).** The
first sidecar was fed the whole log and cannot work: folding the first N events of a
synthetic log costs 4.1 KB per event in the policy (Boat, 3.7 GiB cap: 25k events 125 MB,
200k 843 MB, 800k 3.3 GB, 43 s to start), 7-10 KB per event on the real store copy, and
the full 1.69M-event copy was killed at 6 GiB. The JS hub avoids this by keeping a windowed
state, so the presenter now gets pages and rows, never the log:

| Who | Holds | Size |
|---|---|---|
| Rust (`native/host`) | the log as an offset index (`store.rs`), the read model: per-thread and per-sender entry indexes with `seq` and `ord` (`model.rs`), thread/project metadata, settings, asks, queues | 28 bytes per event; 1M events: 38 MB RSS, listening in 0.73 s; 18.7 bytes of RSS per committed event (JS hub: about 500) |
| Presenter (native Bend, pure) | nothing about the log. Per view request it takes (1) the metadata projection the screen needs (projects, threads, settings, asks, tasks, queues: no entries), (2) the open thread's page as rows, in the cells `Q.Q.rows` reads: `[seq, ord, id, thread, src, kind, role, akind, text, at]`, with `below`/`above`, (3) the request, `now`, and the identity fields | one page is 49 rows (32 shown plus a 17-row lookahead) |

What the presenter returns for one request: `shown`, `session`, `cmds`, `overlay`, and the
`window` (ids, messageIds, historyUnits, contextMessages, partial, anchor, older cursors,
hasMore, remaining, newer, total), because `window` needs the fold and message-context
logic (`FO.Fold.*`, `MC.Context.*`, `LT.Tools.*`: 60+ laws) that stays in Bend. Rust builds
the page, the reply envelope (identity, `viewRevision`, segmentation at 256 KB) and the
keyed deltas; the presenter never sees a connection.

The Rust side is checked against the JS hub's own store: `model.rs` tests compare all 895
entries and 100 pages (every thread, several cursors, cross-thread `t:<thread>:` messages,
long activities, reminders, lone surrogates) with the `store.db` the Bend hub built from the
same log (`test/gpui/hub/fixtures`, regenerated by `make_pages_fixture.mjs`).

**The split of `src/server/thin-view.bend`** (approved on `gpui`, nothing to main). Only 23 of
its 170 defs (133 of about 960 lines) touch the web modules (`A`, `VW`, `WP`, `SS`, and `D`
for the web DOM); the mobile path from `Render.view` reaches 100 defs, 521 lines. The web
renderer is dropped, so the mobile path becomes its own module that the native presenter can
import without `web/app.bend` and `web/view.bend`; `thin-view.bend` keeps the web parts and
calls it for `web = False`, so the JS hub's behaviour is unchanged. Each moved def keeps its
name and its laws; the check is `bend` on both files, the sidebar/thin tests, and an
old-vs-new comparison of `Render.view` over the recorded sessions. PROOF is reported as run
or as not run within the cap, never assumed.

**Targets carried over from the JS hub's measurements (docs/gpui/RESULTS.md):**
- The 20 ms WebSocket RPC floor and the 200 ms `Delta.pace` are not copied blindly. Pacing
  is law-guarded (`pace_*`, `thin_pace_*`): the rule stays, any change to its constants is
  stated here with the law it touches.
- Keyed deltas stay (2.8 KB/s against 84 KB/s per client).
- `overlay.j.projects` repeats `shown.projects` (336 KB per body at 301 threads): byte
  compatible for now, but built once and not stored twice.
- The read model must not grow with the log (the JS hub's RSS floor rises about 500 bytes
  per event): bounded, with a test on Boat (`test/gpui/hub/restart_scale.sh`).
- Restart at 1M events must be small (JS hub: 25.6 s to first view).

Staged as before, each screen compared with the Bend output on recorded traffic: the
presenter first, then the sidebar and status rows into Rust (this also removes the
O(threads^2) sidebar render: 1,000 threads 4.5 s at -O0, 0.5 s in the JS hub).

The phones still run `bridge.js`, Bend's client compiled to JS inside the app. That is
separate from the hub, and it stays until a later decision.

## Milestones

Each milestone ends with its gate passing and its numbers reported. Later milestones
start only when the one before passes, except where noted.

| # | Work | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| M0 | Parity harness; baselines; law map; store copy | Harness replays recorded traffic against today's hub and reports zero differences against itself |
| M1 | Protocol: HTTP, authenticated WebSocket, join, view open/update/action, CBOR frames | Recorded sessions match the JS hub; the phones' `window` and `overlay` fields match; GPUI opens a thread and sends; phone and GPUI clients connect to the Rust hub on the store copy |
| M2 | Event store and windowed history | Same log, same pages; torn tail, restart and 1M-event tests pass |
| M3 | Broadcast and fan-out | Keyed deltas match; slow clients bounded; 20-agent load valid |
| M4 | Threads and sidebar status | Sidebar and settle state match on recorded logs; 300 and 6,666 threads stay fast |
| M5 | Agent process management | Stand-in turns match event for event; interrupt, resume and restart tested |
| M6 | Files and KiCad IPC | Files tab, viewers and `kicad_ipc` match; plots served to all clients |
| M7 | Dropped | The 24 h run was dropped by the person (2026-11-05). Memory flatness is shown by a run of up to about 1 h, if needed |

## Measurement

Every milestone reports against the JS hub on the same store copy and machine:

- Startup to usable
- Steady memory (a simulated run of up to about 1 h, labelled as such)
- CPU under the 20-agent load
- Click and popup latency: sidebar click, thread switch, composer popup
- Build time and peak build memory; binary size

The live hub is only observed, read-only, through `/proc`. Load tests use simulated
agents only, never real ones.

## Laws

`LAWS.bend` holds 3,248 laws and stays as it is: humans own it, and no law is weakened
or deleted. Each law has one of these statuses, listed for every law below:

- **bend**: the logic stays in Bend and Rust calls it. The law still checks that code.
- **rust**: the logic moved. A named Rust property test states the same claim.
- **unported**: the logic has not moved. The law still stands on the Bend code.
- **client**: client-side Bend in the web and phone bundles, unchanged.
- **window**: native-window code GPUI replaces. The entry says what GPUI does instead,
  or that it has no equivalent yet.
- **retired**: web-client-only, gone with the web page. The entry names the law's reason;
  a law that also guards a native client is not retired.

First classification, by the file whose defs a law names (within about 10% until each
law is checked; detail in `PORT-CLASSIFICATION.md`): 1,470 stay in Bend, 963 move to Rust,
581 are client-side and 221 are native-window code. Some client-side laws become
`retired` now that the web page is gone.

A function stays in Bend only if it is pure, takes and returns kilobytes rather than a
state, is off the per-event and per-view hot paths, and has a law or security property
that justifies it. Those rules run in one persistent process that can do no IO. Bend's
`State.replay` stays as a differential oracle: every recorded log must give the same
canonical state digest in Bend and in Rust.

Porting a piece of logic changes its laws from `unported` to `bend` or `rust` in the same
commit. `scripts/gpui/law-map.py` fails when ported code has a law with neither, or when
a named test does not exist. A law with no honest Rust statement stays in Bend, or the
port waits for the person's decision.

## Build and test rules

- Builds run on Boat (8 GiB cap, zero swap) or the Mac, never on the desktop.
- Nothing heavy runs on the desktop: processes there stay under 3.7 GiB. Scale and
  out-of-memory tests run on Boat under a hard cap, on synthetic logs.
- The hub runs on a copy of the event log on its own port, with phone access over the
  tailnet. It never touches `~/.backplane-bend` or port 3787.
- The copy holds real conversations: it stays on this machine, out of git and off
  Boat. Parity recordings sent to Boat come from fixtures only.

## Platforms: macOS and Windows

Today's Bend hub is a JS service on Bun and runs on Linux, macOS (darwin-arm64, darwin-x64) and Windows (windows-x64). The Rust hub must not narrow that.

**macOS** (small). The Linux-only calls are behind `cfg(target_os = "linux")` with a macOS fallback: `agents/proc.rs` `PDEATHSIG` (macOS has none: the next hub on the home reaps what is left, `reap_orphans`; off Linux it does nothing for now, because `ps eww` text matching can kill the wrong process (mark quoted in an argv, a home with a space). To do: read each process's environment with `sysctl(KERN_PROCARGS2)` and compare the exact entry, same uid only, or keep `<home>/agents.pids` with start times; test: mark only in argv survives, a home that prefixes another with a space survives), `term/pty.rs` `ptsname_r` (macOS: `ptsname` under a lock), the RSS read in `presenter.rs` (`/proc/<pid>/status`; elsewhere no checkpoint by size, only by event count). `flock`, `setsid`, `getifaddrs`, `posix_openpt` exist on Darwin. Check: `cargo check --bin backplane-hub` on hs-mac-mini under the `mac-heavy` lock PASSES (darwin-arm64, 2026-11-05, warnings only); next the test suite once; then a smoke run (open the viewers, a terminal, a stand-in agent). Effort: about one day plus fixes the compiler finds.

**Windows** (large, not started). The Rust hub is unix-only in these places: `hub.lock` uses `flock` (Windows: `LockFileEx` on the same file); process groups and signals (`setsid`, `killpg`, `SIGKILL`, `kill(pid)`: Windows needs Job Objects, `TerminateJobObject`, `taskkill`-free); terminals are a pty (`posix_openpt`; Windows: ConPTY via `CreatePseudoConsole`); unix sockets for KiCad IPC (nng over named pipes or TCP loopback); `libc` ioctl/winsize; `/` path assumptions in the file lane (`Fm.safe`, `threadpath`); shell scripts under `files/scripts/*.sh` and the `sh -c` runners (Windows: PowerShell or a bundled busybox); `proc.rs`'s Kind rules (program names `curl`, `sh`) need `.exe`. Approach: first a `platform/` module (`lock`, `process`, `pty`, `ipc`) with the unix implementation moved behind it and a Windows implementation of each, then a `windows-x64` cross-check build in CI (cargo check, no run), then runs on a Windows box. Effort: about 3-4 weeks for one lane (lock + process 3 days, ConPTY 1 week, KiCad IPC transport 4 days, scripts and paths 1 week, tests and CI the rest). Decision needed from the person: whether the Rust hub ships on Windows at the same time as Linux and macOS, or the Bend hub keeps Windows until it is done.

### Retired laws (web client only)

Decision: native apps only, no web page. A law is retired only when every file it names is web-only (a src/web/*.bend file outside the phone bundle's imports); a law that also names a native-shared file is never retired, and when unsure it stays `client`.

None seeded.

Laws that name a web-only file but also native-shared code, so kept (review by hand):

- `stop_command_only_while_running` names `src/web/workspace.bend`
- `terminal_empty_input_keeps_view` names `src/web/workspace.bend`
